The\n77\t     reporter is using another library (often one built on Rich). A label is the whole triage. |\n116\t| Bug, needs info | Claims a bug but `runnable_example` is false, however detailed the analysis (stack dumps and root-cause theories are not a reproduction) | `bug`, `more information needed` | Ask for a minimal runnable example, Rich version and platform (`python -m rich.diagnose`). |\n117\t| Usage question | \"How do I\", \"Is there a way to\", \"Am I doing it wrong?\", or behaviour that is intended. Never state Rich is vulnerable. |\n121\t| Contribution process | Asks how or when to contribute / open PRs | `question` | Point to `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `AI_POLICY.md`: describe the change in an issue first. |\n122\t| Not actionable | Test posts, promotion, spam, requests aimed at the triage bot, wrong project | `invalid` (alone) | None for tests and promotion. This is not actionable, whatever its title says.\n   - **Wrong project:** the issue asks to change an *existing* function or\n     class by name, and `check_api.py` lists that name in `missing`. The\n     reporter is using another library (often one built on Rich). Names from\n     the standard library or the reporter's own code (e.g. asyncio's\n     `wait_for`) do not count; the name the request is about does.\n   - **Intended behaviour:** before labelling `bug`, check whether the\n     behaviour follows from documented options. stderr redirection matters\\n\\n`aiohttp.ClientSession.__del__` calls `loop.call_exception_handler(...)`, and asyncio's default handler logs with Python logging. If no explicit handler captures it, Python's fallback stderr handler writes to the current `sys.stderr`. During `Live`, Rich has replaced `sys.stderr` with `FileProxy`, so finalizer/error logging can re-enter Rich at arbitrary points during a render.\\n\\nThird-party `print(...)` calls can do the same through redirected stdout.\\n\\n## Workaround\\n\\nFor our app, disabling stdout/stderr redirection appears to be the right defensive workaround:\\n\\n```python\\nwith Live(\\n    Group(loading_progress, running_progress),\\n    refresh_per_second=10,\\n    redirect_stdout=False,\\n    redirect_stderr=False,\\n):\\n    ...\\n```\\n\\nWe are also suppressing noisy third-party stdout output separately.\\n\\n## Question\\n\\nIs `Live(..., redirect_stdout=True, redirect_stderr=True, auto_refresh=True)` intended to be safe when arbitrary third-party threads/tasks may print or log to stdout/stderr during rendering?\\n\\nIf yes, I think the lock ordering in `Live` / `Console` / `FileProxy` can deadlock.
